2. RECOGNITION 
       A.  2015–2016 National Merit Scholarship Semifinalists  
This year, more than one million students, nationwide, from some 22,000 high schools, 
entered the National Merit Scholarship Program; however, only 16,000 of these seniors 
are selected to be semifinalists.  This evening, we will be passing a resolution to 
commend and congratulate our sixty-eight National Merit Scholarship Semifinalists. This 
is a remarkable number of semifinalists and a tribute to the staff and parents of the 
Troy School District.  Copies of this resolution were presented to the students at a 
reception on October 14th. 
RECOMMENDATION:  Adopt the following resolution. 
   MOVED BY __________________________ 
   SUPPORTED BY ________________________

2015-2016  
National Merit Scholarship Semifinalists 
Whereas, the following sixty-eight students earned the prestigious honor of being 
among those selected, nationwide, as this year's National Merit Scholarship 
Semifinalists:  Swapnil Akunuri, Sujai Arakali, Ajay Balaji, Benjamin Balas, Sirisha Billa,

Whereas, student achievement is honored through the National Merit Scholarship 
semifinalist and finalist recognition programs; and 
Whereas, National Merit students reach semifinalist status by scoring in the top one 
and one-half percent on the National Preliminary Scholastic Aptitude Test (PSAT) and 
earning comparable scores on the Scholastic Aptitude Test (SAT). 
Be It Therefore Resolved that the Troy School District Board of Education adopts 
this resolution in honor of the aforementioned Troy School District students for their 
selection as 2015-2016 National Merit Scholarship Semifinalists. 
Signed this twentieth day of October, 2015.

2. RECOGNITION 
B. TSD Mission Moment – Troy High Student/Build On 
Tonight, as part of our Troy School District Mission moment, we recognize 
Nithya (Nith-ee-ya) Ramadurai for her efforts to make the world a better 
place here in Troy, across the Metro Detroit region and as far away as Haiti. 
As a 17-year-old senior, Nithya is president of the Build On chapter at Troy 
High School. Build On is an organization that empowers yo uth to transform 
their neighborhoods and the world through intensive community service. 
Nithya volunteers monthly here in Metro Detroit and is a state 
representative for the entire organization. But what really transformed her 
was the opportunity to travel  to Haiti with Build On, on a mission to build 
schools for students there. There are only enough schools to serve a 
quarter of the school age population in Haiti, and Nithya is helping to 
change that. 
That experience was her first exposure to the plight o f underprivileged in 
third-world countries and solidified her desire to spend her life giving back . 
Nithya plans a future caree r with Doctors without Borders where she can 
use her skills and talents to help those struggling with health issues across 
the globe. 
Her accomplishments at the age of 17 are too numerous to mention, but 
tonight we honor Nithya for her hard work and dedication for those less 
fortunate. 
RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Jesse Allg eier, counselor at Troy High, will 
introduce Nithya.

Student Representatives for Board 
student representatives to the Board for the 2015-2016 school 
year: 
Maxwell Fanroy, Athens High School - Max is a well-rounded, 
accomplished and driven senior at Athens High School who thrives 
on academically challenging himself and actively participating in 
the community. Max is a Summa Cum Laude , AP Scholar and active 
member of the National Honors Society. He is also a leader on the 
Debate and Model UN teams as well as an honored Scholar 
Athlete for Power-Lifting and Track & Field. This year, Max is 
exploring career options in Biological Sciences, Medicine and 
Engineering.  He hopes to combine his interests in medicine and 
technology and is currently applying to the University of Michigan, 
Johns Hopkins, Columbia, and the University of Pennsylvania.  
Victoria Malkoun, International Academy East - Victoria is 
currently a junior at IA-East and is the Vice-President of Student 
Council.  Victoria plays club soccer and is an active member of the 
Drama Club.  Victoria is looking at pursuing future careers in either 
law or the sciences when she graduates from the IA.

Michigan Association of School 
Boards’ Awards: Board of 
Education, President Nancy 
Philippart and Trustees Ida 
Edmunds, Paula Fleming, and Gary 
Troy School District Board of Education with several honors that 
had been received last year as a result of its members’ 
commitment to excellence in governance. The Board of Education 
was recognized for its Honor Board achievement and its receipt of 
a Standard of Excellence Award, both of which are presented by 
the Michigan Association of School Boards. Also honored this 
evening, was Board President, Dr. Nancy Philippart for achieving 
the Master Diamond Award and Trustee Gary Hauff for achieving 
the Master Platinum Award. Secretary Paula Fleming was 
recognized for serving 12 years on the Board as well as Gary Hauff 
who has served for 14 years.  Trustee Ida Edmunds was recognized 
for 30 years of service to the Board. All of these honors place the 
Troy Board of Education as being among a handful of other 
Michigan school district governing bodies that have demonstrated 
a true commitment to student achievement and exemplary 
leadership. The Troy community is fortunate to have seven Board 
members who are so committed to the concept of life-long 
learning and positive policy making.  Board members received a 
certificate or pin for their achievement.  Mrs. Edmunds received a 
special plaque engraved with her years of service.

Outstanding Superintendent 
Communicator Award – Dr. Rich 
Machesky, as the recipient of the 2015 Outstanding 
Superintendent Communicator Award. This award is presented by 
the Michigan School Public Relations Association (MSPRA). The 
Outstanding Superintendent Communicator award annually 
recognizes a practicing superintendent of schools or CEO of an 
education agency, for outstanding leadership in school public 
relations and communications. Entries were received from across 
Michigan and were judged by a panel of veteran communications 
professionals.  Dr. Machesky was singled out for the OneTROY 
initiative, utilizing multiple methods of communication and his 
efforts to create an effective, open line of dialogue with the Troy 
community. MSPRA Board Member Rebecca Fannon was in  
attendance at this evening’s meeting to present the award to Dr. 
Machesky.  The Board congratulated Dr. Machesky on his award. 
TSD Mission Moment – Student 
Mural Project for Troy Children’s 
teachers and students, who came together to create beautiful, 
one-of-a-kind art murals for the new DMC Children’s Hospital in 
Troy. The artwork will bring joy to countless children who may be 
undergoing medical procedures or treatment in our community. 
We are extremely proud of our students and staff who put 
countless hours and immense talent into this project.  The Board 
also welcomed the art director from Children’s Hospital, Grace 
Serra, who worked with our teachers and students throughout this 
wonderful project.  C & I Specialist of Fine Arts, Carrie Peyok and 
Art teacher, Katie Hosbach then addressed the Board and audience 
to share a little more about what went into the murals and the 
importance of the endeavor. Teachers and students were called up 
to receive a certificate following the presentation.

Staffing Update 
Jordan Harris, Assistant Superintendent, Employee Services, 
provided a brief update on teacher staffing for the 2015/2016 
school year.  He mentioned the factors that influenced the number 
of teachers hired, which included retirements/resignations, full-
year leaves of absence, FTE status, and student enrollment.  He 
also indicated that the hiring process was almost complete and 
that only a couple of positions still needed to be filled.   
CURRICULUM 
Teaching & Learning Update 
Mark Dziatczak, Deputy Superintendent of Teaching and Learning 
and Kris Griffor, Assistant Superintendent, Elementary Instruction, 
provided and update on the teaching and learning department and 
the collaboration being done to deepen student learning.  They 
talked about the focus and values of teaching and learning and 
engaging students.  They reviewed strategies, their goals, and the 
work being done to cultivate a collaborative culture within the 
OneTroy community.   
Dr.  Philippart shared that the Board had an opportunity to review 
all the work of the teaching and learning department during the

It was moved by Mr. Schmidt and supported by Ms. Underwood 
that BE IT THEREFORE RESOLVED  that the Board of Education 
last workshop and that she was very impressed with the quality of 
the work that was accomplished in such a short time.   
Mr. Dziatczak also shared information about the District’s new 
learning management system, Schoology, and how this system will 
integrate with Office 365 and other apps as well as all devices such 
as iPads, Smartphones, and PC’s.  They indicated that this would 
be a safe, secure, class communication between students and 
teachers.  They explained the benefits of having everything in one 
place and the immediate synchronization and real-time updates 
on grades which will make progress reports no longer necessary.  
They talked about the collaborative environment using Schoology 
would create between students and teachers. 
Mr. Schmidt asked about gradebooks for secondary students.  Mr. 
Dziatczak indicated that teachers will have the ability to hide 
columns so that grades won’t be shown until all student grades 
have been posted.  Mr. Dziatczak and Ms. Griffor thanked Kris 
Young, Director of Technology and Ron Keoleian, C & I Specialist 
for Technology, and the Data Processing staff for their work on 
Schoology.  A few things were discussed between Board members 
and Mr. Dziatczak regarding the Home Access Center (HAC) and 
parent communications.  Mr. Hauff and Mr. Schmidt talked about 
the forward thinking being done and indicated that this was a 
much better tool using one system instead of multiple ones.  Mr. 
Dziatczak said it was a collaborative effort.

A. CONSIDERATION:  Sex Education Advisory Board Committee  
        Membership  
Districts must establish a Sex Education Advisory Board.  At least one -half of the 
board must be parents and one of the parents must co -chair the board.  A 
complete membership list for the 201 5/2016 school year  is attached for your 
consideration and it would be appropriate at this time for the Board of Education 
to adopt the following resolution.

7. CURRICULUM 
C.   REPORT:  Post-Secondary Attainment Update 
Students earning postsecondary credentials prior to graduation from 
high school enjoy significant advantages over their peers without 
those credentials.  They are more likely to be successful in academic 
studies beyond high school and more likely to persis t and obtain a 
postsecondary degree.  Because of this, the Post -Secondary credit 
attainment level for school districts is a key performance indicator of 
the success of the students in a district.   
Consequently, the number and percentage of students parti cipating 
in the traditional high school advanced placement programs are 
reviewed annually. These advanced placement classes can qualify 
students for college waivers and credit upon their matriculation to 
the college or university of their choice.  Additionally, we track the 
annual Equity and Access scores as these statistics further clarify the 
number of graduates successfully taking advantage of advanced 
placement classes. 
Post-secondary opportunities also exist at our alternative high 
schools.  At Niles , students are encourage d to take college classes 
at Oakland Community College prior to graduation through their 
dual enrollment program.  At the International Academy, students 
enroll in challenging courses intended to make them eligible for a n 
IB Diploma, which qualifies them for college waivers and credit at 
colleges and universities similar to advanced placement classes. 
This evening’s report will provide information regarding the Troy 
School District’s progress toward the goal of expanding early 
postsecondary opportunities for as many students as possible. 
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  This evening’s report is presented by 
 Mark Dziatczak, Deputy Superintendent of Teaching and Learning.

 C.  CONSIDERATION:  Athens Chiller Restoration 
 A cooperative bid pricing proposal was requested from Trane U.S. Inc. 
for the refurbishment/restoration of the existing Trane chiller at 
Athens High School.  The objective of the project is to restore the unit 
to manufacturer specification efficiency and performance.  
 RECOMMENDED ACTION:  Accept the resolution below. 
SUGGESTED RESOLUTION 
 Moved by:   
 Supported by: 
Be it therefore resolved that the price proposal from Trane U.S. Inc. in

 Yes: 
 No: 
149
Memorandum 
Department 
FROM:  Rob Carson – Director of Maintenance & Operations   
1140 Rankin 
Troy, MI 48083 
RE:       Athens Chiller Restoration  
Ph: 248.823.4050 
Fax: 248.823.4077 
DATE:   October 2, 2015  
A pricing proposal was requested from Trane U.S. Inc. to obtain cooperative  bid 
pricing for the Refurbishment/Restoration of the existing Trane Chiller at Athens High 
School.  Original bond scope had identified the replacement of this chiller but upon 
further review by the TSD Staff, the Bond Design/Engineering Team and Authorized 
Trane Represent atives it was determined that if this chiller has manufacturer 
restoration performed it will provide an additional 12 -15 years of service life.  It was 
a consensus from all on the team to restore this unit instead of replacing it .  The 
Trane Restoration of  this chiller will restore the unit to manufacturer specification 
efficiency and performance. 
The price proposal from Trane is based on The Cooperative Purchasing Network 
rates.  This cooperative bid network  is widely used by school districts and 
municipalities and we have used this consortium in the past.  
The decision to r estore the Athens Chiller instead of replacing  it results in a savings 
of over $650,000.00.  The total cost of the Trane Chiller Restoration Project is 
$69,736.00.  The funding for this purchase will be from the Capital Project Funds. 
If you have any questions, please let me know.  
Cc:   Pat Orczykowski – Business Department

TCPN QUOTE # 34-207843-15-002 
Subject:  Trane Compressor R’newal™ for  CenTraVac® 
Trane’s CenTraVac® Compressor R’newal service is a major inspection and maintenance program that brings the 
chiller compressor back to like-new condition.  The CenTraVac Compressor R'newal program is backed with a 
Trane warranty covering compressor motor, bearing and lubrication system failures. This unique warranty reflects 
Trane’s confidence in our compressors as well as our factory-authorized service technicians who work on them. 
This CenTraVac Compressor R'newal program addresses the following major components:  
Gaskets and seals – Both internal and external elastomer seals give up elasticity and harden with time. The rate 
of deterioration depends on length of service, temperature, and operating conditions. Gasket and seal wear 
ultimately results in chiller efficiency loss, refrigerant loss and loss of lubrication capability.   
Bearings – Individual unit operating conditions and certain site conditions can influence bearing permanence.  
These factors can result in bearing damage and compressor failure. 
Compressor Motors – Over time compressor motors can develop flaws in the rotor and stator assemblies. These 
flaws, if left uncorrected, can result in compressor motor failure.  
Trane Building Services uses the CenTraVac Compressor Evaluator to predict when a compressor should be 
considered for R’newal.  The CenTraVac Compressor Evaluator takes into account run hours, starts, and seasons 
of operation to derive the service threshold indicator used in this evaluation process.  
We have assessed your CenTraVac compressor with the Evaluator and have determined a service threshold 
indicator of >100%. Trane recommends that any compressor above 85% be considered for the R'newal program 
and compressors above 100% be immediately scheduled for the R’newal program.  
Per the attached proposal, it is our recommendation that your chiller be scheduled for a CenTraVac Compressor 
R'newal service.  
Thank you for giving us this opportunity. If you have any questions or concerns, please call me at (734) 452-2000. 
Sincerely, 
Kathy Taylor

Subject:  Trane CenTraVac R’Newal 
The Trane CenTraVac overhaul services program is a comprehensive program from Trane Building Services, 
designed to prevent refrigerant leaks, compressor failures, and avoid unscheduled downtime. This process 
replaces worn materials, restores compressor performance, and updates applicable components to current 
design.  The Trane CenTraVac overhaul services program delivers Trane’s original commitment of reliability and 
performance and is backed by our OEM support.

Scope of Service - CenTraVac Compressor R'newal Service 
 Refrigerant removal and replacement per applicable law  
 Dismantling of the centrifugal compressor 
 Refrigerant Analysis using Trane Chemical Laboratory* 
 Inspection and verification of the inlet guide vane assembly, motor shaft, labyrinth seals, and the impellers 
compared to Trane specifications*. 
 Motor inspection including a rotor bar and resistance analysis of the motor to Trane specifications*  
 Verification and adjustment of the controls and measuring devices* 
 Inspection of the overload controls, contactors, wiring, and other starter components*.  
 Cleaning and inspection of the lubrication system including the oil pump, regulator, filters, heating elements, 
and sump* 
 Cleaning and inspection of economizer and liquid line flanges (recommend repair as necessary*)  
 Installation of new Trane compressor motor bearings 
 Speed balance the rotor and impellers as one operating assembly prior to reassembly of the compressor  
 Reassembly of the centrifugal compressor, auxiliary vapor and liquid lines, and sight glasses with all new 
Trane gaskets 
 Installation of motor terminal board using new Trane approved gaskets and terminal O -rings 
 Replace relief valve carbon disk and gaskets 
 Chiller evacuation and leak testing to Trane specifications 
 Charge with refrigerant and adjust charge as necessary (any additional refrigerant required must be provided 
by the Owner. Owner's approval will be required if refrigerant cleaning or additional refrigerant is needed.)  
 Start-up and operation check by certified Trane technician 
 Verification of operating parameters and adjustment of the chiller as per its original specifications 
 Trane extended warranty on compressor motor, bearings, and lubrication system  
 Installation of Trane R'newal nameplate indicating Trane issues CenTraVac compressor R’newal serial 
number. 
 Factory parts and labor warranty on compressor – see Warranty section (below) for details 
* Evaluations will be by Trane representative. Any required additional repairs will be brought to your 
attention, quoted separately, and will be done only following your approval.  
CenTraVac Compressor Motor R’newal Warranty 
2 year Compressor Motor Warranty – A Trane Parts and Labor warranty certificate will be issued covering 
compressor motor failure, motor bearing failure, and lubrication system failure. This warranty is contingent upon a 
Trane Service Agreement (described below) being in continuous effect from the date of R’newal completion 
throughout the extended warranty period and that all required repairs identified by Trane representatives are 
satisfactorily performed. 
CenTraVac compressor R’newal warranties require the purchase and maintenance of a Service 
Agreement that includes, at a minimum, an annual chiller inspection, start -up inspection, run inspection, 
and an annual oil analysis for the duration of the extended warranty period.  Cancellation of the service 
agreement voids the R’newal warranty. 
For all other content, our warranty is one-year parts and 90 days labor

circumstances as of the time Company performs the Services.   Company is not liable for any claims, damages, losses, or expenses, arising

agreed to by Customer and Company, at Customer’s expense and before the Services begin, Cus tomer will provide any necessary access 
platforms, catwalks to safely perform the Services in compliance with OSHA or state industrial safety regulations.

and

Services is warranted to have been properly performed for a period of 90 days from date of completion (the "Warranty") .  Company obligations 
of equipment start-up, if any are stated in the Proposal, are coterminous with the Warranty period. Defects must be reported to Company within 
the Warranty period. Company’s obligation under the Warranty is limited to repairing or replacing the defective part at its option and to 
correcting any improperly performed labor/labour. No liability whatsoever shall attach to Company until the Services have been paid for in full.  
Exclusions from this Warranty include damage or failure arising from: wear and tear; corrosion, erosion, deterioration; Customer's failure to 
follow the Company-provided maintenance plan; and modifications made by others to Company's equipment.  Company shall not be obligated 
to pay for the cost of lost refrigerant or lost product.  Additional terms and conditions of warranty coverage are applicable  for refrigeration 
equipment.  Some components of Company equipment may be warranted directly from the component supplier, in which event this Company 
Warranty shall not apply to those components but shall be pursuant to the warranty given by such component supplier. Notwiths tanding the 
foregoing, all warranties provided herein terminate upon termination or cancellation of this Agreement.  Equipment, material and/or parts that 
are not manufactured by Company are not warranted by Company and have such warranties as may be extended by the respective 
manufacturer.

12.  Force Majeure.  Company’s duty to perform under this Agreement is contingent upon the non-occurrence of an Event of Force Majeure.   
If Company shall be unable to carry out any material obligation under this Agreement due to an Event of Force Majeure, this Agreement shall 
at Company’s election (i) remain in effect but Company’s obligations shall be suspended until the uncontrollable event termin ates or (ii) be 
terminated upon ten (10) days notice to Customer, in which event Customer shall pay Company for all parts of th e Services furnished to the 
date of termination.  An "Event of Force Majeure" shall mean any cause or event beyond the control of Company.  Without limiting the foregoing, 
“Event of Force Majeure” includes: acts of God; acts of terrorism, war or the public enemy; flood; earthquake; lightning; tornado; storm; fire; civil 
disobedience; pandemic insurrections; riots; labor disputes; labor or material shortages; sabotage; restraint by court order or public authority 
(whether valid or invalid), and action or non-action by or inability to obtain or keep in force the necessary governmental authorizations, permits, 
licenses, certificates or approvals if not caused by Company and the requirements of any applicable government in any manner that diverts 
either the material or the finished product to the direct or indirect benefit of the government.

C.  2015/2016 Superintendent Annual Report & Goals 
The Troy School District had a very  successful year in 201 4/2015. The 
excellence of our staff and students continues to be recognized at national 
and state levels and we are committed to making necessary 
improvements to ensure the success of every student. The following 
presentation will provide a brief overview of our  Annual Report and 
Superintendent Goals for this school year.   
RECOMMENDED ACTION: 
This report will be given by Dr. Machesky.
